
# SUMMARY

The failure is caused by a TypeError: "Cannot read properties of null (reading 'useCallback')" during Next.js static prerendering. This error typically occurs when a React hook (like useCallback) is called inside a component or function that is not within a valid React context, or when you try to use hooks on a value that is null.

**Solution Steps:**

1. **Audit your pages/components:** 
   - Check the components rendered for the following pages (as listed in the logs):  
     `/social/forums`, `/social`, `/social/learning-partners`, `/social/mentorship`, `/social/network`, `/social/peer-matching`, `/tasks/ai-suggestions`, `/tasks/analytics`, `/tasks`, `/team/analytics`, `/tasks/projects`, `/team/collaboration`, `/team/dashboard`, `/team`, `/team/skills`, `/team/mentorship`, `/team/social`, `/team/workflows`, `/workflow/advanced`, `/workflow/automation`, `/workflow/calendar`, `/workflow/marketplace`, `/workflow`, `/workflow/notes`, `/workflow/optimization`, `/workflow/prioritization`, `/digital-twin/team-coordination`.

2. **Look for improper hook usage:**  
   - Make sure you are not calling React hooks inside non-component functions or outside the top level of functional components.
   - Check that any context or provider required for hooks is present in the component tree.

3. **Check for null values:**  
   - If you are destructuring or getting a value from context, props, or state, ensure you check for null before using hooks like `useCallback`.

**Example Code Fix:**
If you have code like this inside a component:
```js
const context = useMyContext();
const handler = useCallback(() => {
  // ...
}, []);
```
But `context` can be null, make sure to guard against it:
```js
const context = useMyContext();
const handler = context ? useCallback(() => {
  // ...
}, []) : null;
```
Or, avoid using hooks when the context is null:
```js
const context = useMyContext();
if (!context) return null;
// Safe to use hooks now
const handler = useCallback(() => { /* ... */ }, []);
```

**References and Next Actions:**
- Review the affected page components for any `useCallback` usage and ensure it’s not being called when a required value is null.
- For more details, see the Next.js prerender error docs: https://nextjs.org/docs/messages/prerender-error

If you want to identify the exact files and lines, please specify which page(s) you want to inspect first, or request a search for `useCallback` usage in your repo.
